- [x] 2026-09-11 [supersession-ignores-coverage-subject] **DONE 2026-10-02: per-item currency, migration 072; the three wrongly superseded server documents (29, 199, 208) wait for a reopen and an approval after deploy.** `L · RULED 2026-09-14, spec first` **RULING (Denis, 2026-09-14): per-item supersession.** A newer declaration replaces an older one **only for the articles it names**, only when genuinely newer, and only within the same type and regulation; the document turns `superseded` only when no production link of its own survives. His reasoning, which settles the cheaper alternative: *"we can't request a manufacturer to always cover all the same devices — what if some go out of production?"* Requiring the newer document to name every article of the older one would treat ordinary product lifecycle as an anomaly. Measured the same day over every pair of production documents sharing a group, type and regulation — 41 pairs: **11 identical article sets, 1 superset, 1 partial overlap, 28 with no shared article**, so the group is the wrong subject 68% of the time. Work: `item_document` gains a link-level `superseded` status and `superseded_by`; C6 amended from "coverage subject" to the article set; **every reader of `document.status='superseded'` must learn the link-level state** (the compliance card, `/documents`, the item page, `/staging`'s unpublished-links panel, `item_document_production`, `bc_fields`) or a screen shows a document as current for articles it no longer covers. Spec before code. Original entry follows. **A declaration naming fewer articles supersedes a wider one outright, and 94 items lost their current DoC.** 2026-08-21: GATE superseded doc 162 (IVOCLAR, cert `G15 043306 0282`, 78 production links over 23 groups) with doc 815 (`ivoclar-doc-2026.pdf`, 1 item, 1 group). 2026-08-19: doc 415 (KOMET, 18 links over 10 groups) by doc 377 (15 links). `_current_production_doc` (`app/handlers/validate.py:675-694`) takes any production document sharing ONE group with the candidate as "the current one"; `validate.py:1043-1044` sets `supersedes`; `_apply_supersession` (`app/handlers/gate.py:679-694`) re-checks only (type, regulation) and supersedes the whole document. Invariant 5 and AC8 require an identical coverage subject. The 96 old links stay `production` but `item_document_production` drops them, so the items fall back to manufacturer scope silently (this is why the 09-04 state's 1.900 DoC figure is 1.806 through the view). Ruling needed: supersede per item link (only the links the successor also covers), or refuse when the successor's subject is narrower and route to manual. Then repair 162/415, which are append-only by invariant 4, via the chosen mechanism, and add a test where the successor covers fewer groups. **Re-read later on 2026-09-11, two corrections.** (1) Doc 815 is not a supplier document: it came from `tools/seed_fake_mailbox.py` (job 35279, source `email:<seed-reply@ivoclar.example>`), so 77 of the 94 items were lost to a dev test email; docs 832 and 839 are seeded copies of the same file. (2) All four supersessions in the DB are wrong, not two. KOMET 375 (533047), 379 (533049), 415 (533079) and 377 (533048) are four different RA 810 declarations, and the chain 375→415, 379→415, 415→377 joins documents that share a catalogue group, never an article (415 ∩ 377 = 0 items). PRD C6 itself says "overlapping coverage subject" (`docs/dentalia-pipeline-contract-prd-v3.md:431`), which is what the code implements and what contradicts invariant 5's "identical". **Recommended, awaiting Denis:** per-link supersession. `item_document` gains a `superseded` status and `superseded_by`; a document becomes `superseded` only when no production link remains; C6 amended. Repair: one audited correction returning 162, 375, 379, 415 and 377 to production and 815, 832, 839 to `rejected`. **Server, measured 2026-09-30 (read-only):** the same defect on the client database from waves 1-3 (2026-09-28): doc 275 "IPS e.max Ceram Art" (11 items) superseded doc 29 "IPS e.max Ceram" (142 items, 0 shared, 2 groups shared), so those 142 items lost their current IVOCLAR MDR DoC; doc 584 (12 items) superseded doc 208 (61 items, 0 shared) in the same second a concurrent GATE for 583 (covers all 61) re-pointed the chain, leaving two `supersede` audit rows on 208; 199 -> 505 is correct; 226 auto-superseded under 29. Wave 4 (Dentsply, VOCO, 3M, Kerr) will add more until the spec lands.

- [x] 2026-10-02 [bc-odata-full-read-times-out] **CLOSED 2026-10-05: the live dry run completed.** Job 23729 (2026-10-02, after 4bd2c65 was deployed) read all **20.027** items in ~3 min 50 s by the worker log (first page ~60 s, then ~8 s a page), not the ~19 min extrapolated below; result seen 20.027, unchanged 15.801, changed 780, non_md 3.446, skipped 0. Original entry: **`bc_client.get` now sends `Prefer: odata.maxpagesize=1000` and the read timeout is 120 s; closes when a dry run against live BC completes.** Measured live 2026-10-02: ~0.058 s per record regardless of `$select` (BC's processing, not transfer), so a full read is ~19 minutes; content on one 1.000-record page matches the mirror (758 held, all same manufacturer). The lasting fix is a delta read on `systemModifiedAt` (deployment.md § 6, "There is no delta"). **The first full live BC read failed: job 23718 (`ingest.run`, `bc_odata`, `dry_run`) timed out reading `allitems` on every attempt (`ReadTimeout`, 30 s).** `app/adapters/bc_client.py` sets `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_S = 30.0` and the request asks for no page size, so BC builds the whole catalogue as one response; the 2026-09-25 check read 200 items and passed. Fix: send `Prefer: odata.maxpagesize=<n>` (the adapter already follows `@odata.nextLink`) and a longer read timeout, then re-run the dry run. Writes nothing; the dead job shows on Today's Failed tasks.

### Closed 2026-09-14 — the fifth readiness audit

- [x] 2026-09-14 [deploy-check-blind-to-web] **DONE 2026-09-14.** `app/version.py` hashes a third root, `web/` (`*.py`, `*.html`, `*.css`, `*.js`; binary assets under `web/static` excluded). `_SOURCES` now carries several patterns per root, since a template-only change is a change the user sees. `scripts/deploy.sh` compares it, treats `ABSENT` as legitimate for a root an image does not carry (no `migrations/` in the web image, no `web/` in the worker's `app` stage) and adds `UNKNOWN` — an image whose own `version.py` never heard of a root predates it, so it is stale rather than waved through. Proven on the stale image before the fix (`<-- STALE app/`, `web=ABSENT`), then `./scripts/deploy.sh` rebuilt, migrated, restarted and verified all three roots. Five tests in `test_version.py`, including one that pins the root list itself. `/missing` answers 200 live.
- [x] 2026-09-14 [bc-push-preview-cert-join-unscoped] **DONE 2026-09-14.** The bulk preview no longer has a query of its own: `app/handlers/bc_push.py` exports `HOLDINGS_FOR_MANY_SQL` from the same `_HOLDINGS_SELECT` the stage uses, so the SRN-scoped, revision-aware certificate join is shared and only the WHERE clause differs. Four tests in `test_bc_web.py`, three of which failed on the old code; the fourth asserts the preview and the push compute the same fields for one item, which is the property that outlives either query. Was: 55 of 123 production documents with a `cert_number` matched a foreign `actor_srn` in the preview, touching 640 production-linked items.

Original entry follows. **GC EUROPE's SRN `BE-MF-000001608` appears in 126 documents we already hold, and `manufacturer_srn` has zero rows for it.** So the article-probe deadlock that entry describes is not the binding problem for GC: the SRN is sitting in text we parsed. `manufacturer_srn` is filled only from the EUDAMED certificate register (`register-exact` / `register-fuzzy`), and a manufacturer with no certificates never appears there — GC has 356 MD articles and zero certificates. What changed is the alternative: GC EUROPE's SRN sits in 126 documents we already hold (`[gc-europe-srn-is-in-our-own-documents]`, whose regex was wrong — see there), so GC can be bootstrapped without the probe at all. Running the entry's own `[0-9]{12}` against `document_text` returns zero rows, so anyone implementing this as written would have measured an empty result and concluded the SRNs were not there. **Re-measured with the correct pattern, live, 2026-09-09: 27 distinct (manufacturer, SRN) pairs.** The ones with no `manufacturer_srn` row: GC EUROPE N.V. `BE-MF-000001608` (126 docs), VOCO `DE-MF-000008601` (108), NEODENT `BR-MF-000014512` (4), HENRY SCHEIN `US-MF-000021683` (2), plus 3SHAPE TRIOS A/S and EDENTA. What changed is that the deadlock no longer BINDS. GC EUROPE, the manufacturer the probe was written for and named in its own docstring, now has a pending SRN read out of document 1, and five more manufacturers came with it. Giving the probe a button would have been a second network path to an answer already sitting in our archive. The residue is real but much smaller than this entry claimed: 340 manufacturers carry item groups with no trusted SRN, mining reaches 6, and most of the other 334 could not be bootstrapped by the article probe either — it needs an exact article match on page 0 of the EUDAMED device register. If that residue ever needs attacking, file it as "the probe has no entry point", which is a much smaller claim than this entry's title. Original entry follows. **The article probe that bootstraps an SRN for a certificate-less manufacturer cannot be reached through any UI or scheduler path.** `app/handlers/eudamed.py:749` runs `probe_srn` only when `trusted_manufacturer_srn` is empty for that manufacturer. `web/registry.py:1626`, the release button and the only production entry point to `eudamed.sweep`, refuses with HTTP 400 on exactly that condition. The scheduler only proposes manufacturers already in the table. The code comment at `eudamed.py:751-753` names **GC EUROPE** as the case the probe exists for, and GC EUROPE carries **356 MD items, zero certificates, no SRN row and no `eudamed_sweep_state` row** — measured 2026-09-04. Only a direct CLI or job-table enqueue bypassing the web validation exercises it today. Either let the release button through when `srn_probed_at IS NULL`, or give the probe its own button.

Both prompts now say `ISO` means **ISO 13485 or ISO 9001 specifically** and name 27001 / 14001 / 45001 as `other` — `t1_text.md` and `t2_vision.md` together, because the vision prompt carried the identical gap and fixing one would have left the tier that reads scanned documents still conflating. `_ISO_STANDARD_NUMBERS` deliberately NOT widened: accepting 27001 would have silenced the flag on exactly the document it correctly caught. Doc 209 re-extracted for **$0,0022** and is now `other`; `validity_from`, `validity_to`, `cert_number`, `coverage_scope`, `canonical_manufacturer` and `status` all unchanged. **Swept for others the new rule would re-type: none.** No settled document typed `ISO` names an ISO/IEC standard other than 13485 or 9001, so the population was exactly the one document. Original entry follows. **Doc 209 is an ISO/IEC 27001 information-security certificate, and the prompt and the flag disagree about what it is.** Found by running the type backfill 2026-09-09: 248, 260 and 316 corrected `ISO` -> `EC`, and 209 stayed `ISO` through a fresh T1 call on the fixed prompt. T1 is not obviously wrong — `app/extract/prompts/t1_text.md:36-39` says `ISO` is "a certificate issued against the **ISO standard itself**", and 27001 IS an ISO standard; the clause that would exclude it is the next half-sentence, "it always names the standard number: ISO 13485 or ISO 9001", which reads as a description of the corpus rather than as a closed set. `tiers._ISO_STANDARD_NUMBERS = ("13485", "9001")` treats it as closed, so `iso-without-standard-number` DOES fire on 209 — verified directly against its stored fields 2026-09-09. So the two halves disagree, and the document sits at `staged` typed `ISO` with a flag saying nothing supports that. **The substantive question is not the wording, it is scope**: an information-security management certificate is not medical-device compliance evidence at all — it belongs to `other`, the way `[completeness-card]`'s requirement matrix treats non-MD documents. A 9001 certificate raises the same question one step milder. Cheapest close once ruled: state in the prompt that `ISO` means a QMS certificate against 13485 or 9001 specifically and that any other ISO/IEC standard is `other`, then re-extract 209 alone (~$0,002). Do NOT widen `_ISO_STANDARD_NUMBERS` to accept 27001 — that would silence the flag on exactly the document it correctly caught.

  ```python
  # docker compose exec -T test python3 -  (swap DENSTPLY for NEODENT / VOCO)
  import pathlib, warnings; warnings.filterwarnings("ignore")
  from app.extract import t0_layout, t0_templates as t0, pdf as pdfutil
  import pymupdf
  root = pathlib.Path("imports/dentalia-sftp/DENSTPLY")
  allrefs = set()
  for p in sorted(root.rglob("*")):
      if p.suffix.lower() != ".pdf": continue
      try: doc = pymupdf.open(p)
      except Exception: continue
      tpl = t0_layout.match(pdfutil.first_page_text(doc)); doc.close()
      if not tpl: continue
      allrefs |= set((t0.extract_ref_list(p, template=tpl) or {}).get("value") or [])
  print(len(allrefs))
  ```

  **The lesson is the gate, not the tables.** A verification script whose red state is normal stops being read: `deploy.sh --check` had been reporting NOT VERIFIED since 2026-09-04 on schema drift that was nobody's migration. Scratch belongs in a scratch database or a `TEMP` table, never in `dentalia`. Approved by Denis 2026-09-09.

The flag has therefore never been raised in production on a real case. Same shape as `[expiry-flag-should-be-about-evidence]`: the rule is right and the wiring cannot reach it. Fix is to move `flags.extend(tiers.integrity_flags(fields))` above the mfr-binding return so BOTH routes carry extraction-shape flags — `ref-list-possibly-truncated` is equally unreachable there today — with a test that seeds a manufacturer-scope `ISO` document naming no standard number and asserts the flag on the emitted candidate. Both flags are in `gate.INFORMATIONAL_FLAGS`, so nothing that auto-writes today changes disposition; this only makes the counts real. Found by revalidating `[type-fix-never-backfilled]` 2026-09-09.

Per-slug and explicit by design — a blanket "files win" would revert every UI edit. Part (1) was already closed by `scripts/deploy.sh` on 2026-09-03. Verified 2026-09-09: `./scripts/deploy.sh --check` reports `worker app=9e0047a33738 ok` and `web app=9e0047a33738 ok` against the tree, so neither running image is stale. The probe/wizard remains the nicer close for authoring, but it is no longer the ONLY way in and this entry is no longer describing the tree. Original entry follows. **A green test suite can coexist with a pipeline running last week's code, and nothing anywhere says so.** Two independent staleness traps, both hit in one session. (1) **Only the `test` service bind-mounts the repo** (`docker-compose.yml:445-446`, `.:/app`). `worker`, `scheduler` and `web` run the baked `dentalia-app:latest`. Measured 2026-09-03: the image was built 2026-09-02T18:55 and `docker compose exec worker` raised `'Playbook' object has no attribute 'crawl'` for code committed hours earlier, while the same import succeeded in the `test` container. Nothing in `docs/runbook.md` or CLAUDE.md tells you to rebuild, and CLAUDE.md's test section actively says the opposite for the container it is talking about ("the repo is bind-mounted, so rebuild by hand only when the Dockerfile or the dependency set changes") -- true of `test`, false of `worker`. (2) **Playbooks are DATABASE-authoritative and there is no CLI path to push a hand-edited file in.** The worker reads bodies from `manufacturer.body` (`app/workers/runner.py:237` `set_source`), not from `playbooks/*.json`. After authoring `edenta.json`, `dentalia playbooks sync` reported only alias counts and wrote no body; `dentalia playbooks drift` classified the file as **DIVERGED** and said "resolve in the editor", and `--apply` would have regenerated the file FROM the database, silently discarding the new recipe. The only route in was calling `web.registry.save_playbook_body` by hand. Fix is two things: say the rebuild requirement in the runbook (and stop CLAUDE.md's bind-mount sentence reading as global), and give `playbooks sync` a documented file-to-database direction -- or, better, close it with the probe/wizard (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-playbook-probe-wizard-design.md`), which exists precisely so a recipe is authored where it is stored. Cross-referenced by `[no-bring-up-rehearsal]` and `[playbooks-sync-count-misleading]`. **HALF CLOSED 2026-09-03.** Part (1), the rebuild gap, is addressed: `scripts/deploy.sh` builds, migrates, restarts and then VERIFIES -- each running image's per-root source digest against the tree's, then `schema-drift` against the database -- exiting 1 with `DEPLOY NOT VERIFIED` on any disagreement, and `docs/runbook.md` now says plainly that `docker compose up -d` does not pick up your code. Confirmed on the spot: before the deploy both `worker` and `web` reported stale; after it, both match. **Part (2) is untouched and still open** -- playbooks remain database-authoritative with no CLI path to push a hand-edited file in, `playbooks sync` still writes no body, and `--apply` would still regenerate the file FROM the database. The probe/wizard is still the proposed close. Do not read this entry as done.

### Closed 2026-09-04 — the UI audit's own findings, worked in order

- [x] 2026-09-04 [probe-browser-tier] **CLOSED 2026-09-04 — built.** Escalates on the SAME trigger the crawl rung uses (§4.2): zero ANCHORS, never zero matches. A page with anchors that matched none has a wrong `link_pattern`, and a browser renders the same anchors and matches none again -- 30s to reprint the same number; a page with NO anchors is the one that might be a JavaScript shell. NOTE the entry's own example, "5 links, 0 matched", is therefore NOT the case this fixes and never should have been: that is a wrong pattern, not a missing browser. A render fault degrades to the static answer and `tier` stays `static`; a render that succeeds but finds nothing reports `rendered`, because "we tried" differs from "we never tried". `_probe.html` has read `probe.tier` since S2.2 and can finally print "Read with a browser". Four tests. Original entry follows: [probe-browser-tier] `S ~1h` `app/handlers/playbook_probe.py` hardcodes `tier="static"` and never escalates to the renderer. The crawl design's rung escalates on ZERO anchors; the probe does not, so a JavaScript-drawn library reports "5 links, 0 matched" rather than "this page needs a browser". `playbook_probe.tier` exists precisely to make "this portal needs a browser" measured rather than authored, and today it can only ever say `static`. Blocked on nothing; deferred because it is a second real fetch per probe and the spec (§4.4) prices it at ~30s.

(2) The conflation: `lost = [f for f in refused if f not in out]` -- the flag fires ONLY where the field went unset, never where a later label recovered it (doc 317). Denis chose that reading on 2026-09-04 ("flag only the lost field"). Pinned by `test_t0.py:1818-1841` and `test_tiers.py:544`. Nothing to decide and nothing to build. Original entry follows: [date-label-not-adjacent-not-computable] `S ~1h to decide, M to build` The second of the two flags in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-label-adjacency-and-type-conflation-design.md` §5 was never built, and the spec understates what it costs. `iso-without-standard-number` shipped because `integrity_flags(fields)` can compute it from the STORED fields. `date-label-not-adjacent` cannot: the adjacency rule REFUSES a pairing and falls through to the next label, so a refusal leaves nothing in `fields` — it is a non-event, invisible downstream. Building it means the extractor emitting the refusal (a counter or a field on the attempt row), not a VALIDATE-side computation. Also unresolved: the flag conflates "refused and recovered from a later label" (doc 317, correct behaviour) with "refused and the field went unset" (the case worth a `t0_layout` template). Decide which of the two is actually wanted before building either.

- [x] 2026-09-03 [crawl-defer-refetches-earlier-recipes] **CLOSED 2026-09-03 by the commit "crawl: one domain lease per host, not per recipe"; moved here 2026-09-04.** Verified rather than taken on the entry's word: `app/handlers/discover.py:938-939` takes every host's lease before any recipe fetches, and both pinning tests exist (`tests/test_discover_crawl.py:699`, `:736`). It sat in `## Open` because "FIXED" was typed into the effort-tag slot instead of the checkbox. **The standing lesson is not lost by closing this** — it is written out in full in `tasks/lessons.md` (§ "a green suite proved nothing about concurrency between two units the tests only ever exercised one at a time"), which cites this entry by slug, and a slug resolves here as well as it did above the line. **A per-recipe domain lease made a multi-library crawl re-fetch its first library forever, and only a live run showed it.** Edenta authors two crawl recipes on ONE host (`edenta.com`): instructions for use, and ISO/EC certificates. Recipe 1 took the lease and fetched; recipe 2 found that same lease held seconds later; the rung deferred the WHOLE job (a deliberate choice made that morning, so a deferred library would not be silently dropped); the retry re-ran recipe 1 **from the top** -- fetch, harvest, emit 0 because all 13 links were already active. **56 live fetches of one manufacturer's document list** before it was stopped by hand (`job 36428`, marked dead). Spaced by the 2s politeness interval so not abusive, but unbounded. **The test suite could not have caught this**: every crawl test used a single recipe, and the defect needs two recipes sharing a host. Fixed by taking ONE lease per host for the whole crawl, before any recipe fetches -- deferring there is safe because nothing has been fetched, which is exactly the property the loop destroyed -- plus an internal `politeness_ms` pause between recipes, since a multi-recipe crawl is one job making several requests and FETCH's per-job spacing does not apply. The lease now also precedes the `robots.txt` read, because that is itself a request to a host we have been asked to wait on. Pinned by `test_two_recipes_on_one_host_do_not_deadlock_each_other` and `test_recipes_on_one_host_are_spaced_by_the_politeness_interval`. **Standing lesson, and the reason this entry is kept: a green suite proved nothing about concurrency between two units the tests only ever exercised one at a time.**

Showing the three is what removed the need to choose. **What survives and is NOT closed:** `/coverage`'s three numbers and `_kpi_board`'s single headline still answer to different definitions, so the KPI board and `/api/kpi` can still disagree with the gap lists. The copy in `onboarding_library.html`, `playbook_detail.html` and both guide languages promises the exclusion works, so they are part of the fix.

### Closed 2026-09-04 — found by verifying every open claim against the tree, the live database and git

_Three entries were open only because nothing had re-read them. Each was fixed by work that closed a different entry, or by a commit that never named this one. The eight entries that were half-true were corrected in place above rather than moved._

- [x] 2026-08-25 [effective-expiry-inherited-never-fires] **CLOSED 2026-09-04 — the first of this entry's two readings was right, and it was fixed on 2026-08-27 without anyone closing the entry.** Re-measured live rather than read: `document_effective_expiry` now returns `staleness` 365, `stated` 279 and **`inherited` 80** over 724 documents carrying an expiry, all 80 with `inherited=true`. The cause was the citation-resolution gap this entry named as the interesting reading, not the too-narrow condition: back-resolution was unreachable code, a 2026-08-27 change fixed it, and one re-gated job took `document_effective_expiry.basis='inherited'` from 0 to 80 — the same numbers `[cert-citation-misses-by-a-revision-suffix]` records on its own closure. The third rung of the expiry ladder has executed on real data. Original entry follows: `S ~1h · measure, then decide` **`document_effective_expiry.inherited` is false for every document in the registry — 602 of 602 with a non-null expiry** (360 filed, 140 production, 97 staged, 4 superseded, 1 rejected; measured 2026-08-25). The view computes it as `d.validity_to IS NULL AND c.validity_to IS NOT NULL`, and `basis` has a matching `'inherited'` branch, so a whole third of the expiry ladder has never executed on real data — only in `tests/test_report_handler.py::test_expiring_documents_includes_docs_inheriting_a_cert_expiry`, which constructs the case by hand. Live `basis` is only ever `stated` (91) or `staleness` (44). Two readings and they need different fixes: either no declaration in the corpus cites a certificate whose expiry resolves (a RESOLVE/citation gap, and the interesting one — the 2026-08-07 commit built cited-certificate resolution precisely for this), or the condition is too narrow because a DoC that states its own `validity_to` can never inherit even when the certificate it cites lapses first. `app/handlers/report.py`'s docstring presents `inherited` as one of three live grounds; on this registry it is documentation of an intention. Measure which reading holds before touching the view.

- [x] 2026-09-03 [cert-views-drifted-from-the-tree] `CLOSED 2026-09-03 -- data fixed by migration 058, guard shipped as `dentalia schema-drift`` **A migration edited in place after it was applied leaves every long-lived database serving the old definition, and NOTHING anywhere reports it -- not the tests, which build from scratch and are green, and not the migration runner, which keys on filename.** Found 2026-09-03 while investigating why `/expiry` served 1.43 MiB. `043_eudamed_cert_views.sql` was applied here 2026-08-26 10:28 UTC; a later commit edited that same file 2026-08-27 10:37 UTC, adding `SELECT DISTINCT` to `certificate_drift_candidate` and digit-normalised revision comparison to `certificate_drift`. `schema_migrations` is keyed on `filename`, so the edit never re-ran and this database served the pre-fix views for **8 days**. `tests/test_eudamed_views.py::test_certificate_drift_candidate_is_distinct_per_bound_article` pins the corrected shape and passes -- it could never catch this, because the defect is not in the tree, it is the gap between the tree and a database. **How it was found, which is the reusable part:** dump `md5(pg_get_viewdef(oid, true))` for every view in the live database and in a leftover pytest database (which is built from the current migrations), then join on name and compare. Exactly two views differed; every other view matched. **Fixed** by `058_cert_view_drift_repair.sql`, which recreates both from 043's current text: `certificate_drift_candidate` 5.650 rows -> 4, `/expiry` 1.43 MiB -> 37.966 bytes, and the whole-view diff is now empty. **What is still open is the guard, not the data.** A scan of `schema_migrations` against `git log` flags 26 applied migrations edited afterwards, but most are comment churn and it cannot tell which edits were semantic -- it is a lead generator, not a check. The real fix is to record a content hash at apply time (`schema_migrations.sha256`, written by `app/db.py`) and a CLI check that reports any applied migration whose file no longer hashes to what was recorded. That is a schema change plus a CLI subcommand plus a runbook line, hence the estimate. Related: `[worker-image-and-playbooks-are-stale-to-the-tree]`, which is the same class -- the tree is not the running system -- for code and playbooks rather than schema. **GUARD BUILT, and NOT the way this entry proposed.** The proposal was a `schema_migrations.sha256` recorded at apply time. Checked before building and rejected on three counts: it sees only ONE cause (a file edited after it was applied); it **cannot be backfilled honestly** -- hashing the files as they stand today would record 043's CORRECTED text against a database serving the OLD view, laundering the exact bug that motivated the work; and it is blind to a hand-run `ALTER`, a partly-restored dump, or an object made directly in psql. Built instead as `app/schema_drift.py` + `python -m app.cli schema-drift`: build the tree's schema in a scratch database, fingerprint both catalogues per object keyed by `(kind, name)` -- columns, views, indexes, constraints, enums, grants -- compare, drop the scratch. ~10s, exit 1 on drift. **It earned itself on the first run**, reporting three `tmp_*` tables (`tmp_ds`, `tmp_neo_refs`, `tmp_voco`, 0 rows each) created by hand in the live database that no migration describes and that a hash scheme could never have seen. Left in place, not dropped -- they are not mine to remove; **someone should confirm they are dead and drop them.** Also recorded: `pg_dump --schema-only | diff` is NOT a usable substitute, measured at ~100 lines of difference between two IDENTICAL schemas because it orders by dependency and stamps a per-session `\restrict` token. Documented in `docs/runbook.md` (§ schema-drift) and `docs/dev/changing-things.md`.

Needed two fixes first, both filed as their own commit: `contacts` was missing from the playbook editor form, and the file->db sync had no way to say "the file is right this time" -- `manufacturers seed --adopt <slug>`. `playbooks drift` went from 6 diverged to 38 in sync. Was: the eight authored contacts are in git and not yet in the registry.
- [x] 2026-09-03 [source-fingerprint-is-meaningless-on-the-web-image] `FIXED 2026-09-03 same day` **`source_fingerprint` is the repo's stale-image detector and it CANNOT be used on the `web` image: it reports a mismatch every single time, stale or not.** It hashes `app/**/*.py` plus `migrations/*.sql` (`app/version.py` `_SOURCES`), but `Dockerfile.web` COPYs only `app/` and `web/` -- no `migrations/`, and not the whole of `app/`. Measured 2026-09-03: **71 files hashed inside the web container against 132 in the tree** (app 71 vs 73, migrations absent vs 59), giving `7d1c0d5dd8bd` on web against `022ebba11371` on host, worker and test. The two numbers are not comparable and never will be, so the documented procedure -- "run it on the host and in the image and compare, different values mean the image is stale" -- yields a permanent false positive for the one image serving the UI. `version.py`'s own docstring names this exact failure: "a fingerprint that changes for a typo is one people learn to ignore". `python -m app.cli version` also does not run there at all (`ModuleNotFoundError: pymupdf`, pulled in via `app.extract.t0_layout` on the CLI import path), so the documented command is doubly unusable and the fingerprint is only reachable by importing `app.version` directly. **Fix is a choice, not a patch:** either scope the fingerprint to what an image actually contains (hash the files present, and report WHICH set was hashed alongside the digest, so two numbers are only ever compared like with like), or give the web image its own fingerprint over `app/` + `web/` and stop pretending one number covers both images. Related: `[worker-image-and-playbooks-are-stale-to-the-tree]`. **Noted while confirming that one: the worker image was current only because ANOTHER session rebuilt it 8 minutes earlier** -- nothing in the deploy or the runbook makes that happen, so `docker compose exec worker python -m app.cli schema-drift` working today is luck, not process. **FIXED.** `version.fingerprint_parts()` reports a digest per source root, and `cli version` prints them, so `app` is compared against `app` and a root the image does not carry reads `not in this image` instead of silently poisoning one combined number. The combined digest is unchanged and still logged by the worker. **The blind spot was hiding a real defect**: measured the same day, the running web image was genuinely two files behind the tree -- `app/schema_drift.py` and `app/handlers/playbook_probe.py` -- and the combined number could not have told anyone, because it differs permanently for the unrelated reason that `migrations/` is absent. Also corrected above: the 33-vs-93 figures in the original entry came from a non-recursive glob and were wrong; the real counts are 71 vs 132. Now enforced by `scripts/deploy.sh`, which compares per root and refuses to report a verified deploy otherwise.
- [x] 2026-09-02 [no-bring-up-rehearsal] `CLOSED 2026-09-03 — and it found two real bring-up defects on its first runs` **Every piece of the deploy sequence is tested and the sequence itself is not.** Nothing — no test, script, or runbook procedure — takes an empty machine through: stack up, migrations, seed, ingest a real BC export, scan a corpus folder, assert the registry landed. `tests/test_compose_config.py` guards config drift and `test_web_import_closure.py` guards the slim image, but neither runs the order. That order is exactly what next week's deploy performs, and two known traps live inside it: `[manufacturer-seed-is-one-shot]` (a fresh environment migrates against an empty `manufacturer_alias` and `manufacturer` stays empty forever) and the archive/`archive_url` path rewrite, which has already needed a repair CLI once. Cheapest form is a documented runbook rehearsal against a throwaway database plus a smoke assertion, not a new test harness. **BUILT as `scripts/bringup-rehearsal.sh`**, in the cheap form this entry asked for: a throwaway database, the real sequence, smoke assertions, no new harness. **It paid for itself immediately, twice.** (1) `manufacturers seed --apply` **hard-fails on an empty database** -- `ForeignKeyViolation: Key (code_source, code)=(LJ, 001) is not present in table "vendor_master"`, because `manufacturer_bc_code` is FK'd to `vendor_master`. The BC vendor master must be imported FIRST and nothing anywhere said so; every test seeds `vendor_master` in a fixture, and the live database was populated in an order nobody wrote down. (2) The seed writes **no aliases at all** -- 381 manufacturers, 0 `manufacturer_alias` rows -- because aliases are `playbooks sync`'s job. A deploy stopping after the seed would report success at every step and then resolve nothing, leaving every group unnamed. That is `[manufacturer-seed-is-one-shot]`'s trap in its real form, sharper than the entry predicted. The verified order is now a table in `docs/runbook.md`. **Passes end to end**: 390 vendor codes, 381 manufacturers, 445 aliases, 15.958 items, 4.265 md_flag, 273 groups all named, no failed or dead jobs. **Still not covered** and deliberately so: no live fetching, so the archive/`archive_url` rewrite -- this entry's other named trap -- remains covered only by its repair CLI. A second lesson worth keeping: the script's own first draft asserted the SYMPTOM ("item_mirror is empty") before the CAUSE (a failed job), and reported the wrong thing for a path misconfiguration the job had already recorded; it now checks job status first.

Never a bare `pytest`" and documents the four invocations, with a PreToolUse hook enforcing it. No host-side `python -m pytest` instruction survives. Original report below.
  `XS ~15m · a contradiction between two instructions, not a bug` `CLAUDE.md` says **"Always run pytest as `python -m pytest -q -n 4 --dist loadfile`"** -- a HOST-side command -- while a 2026-08-27 change removed the host port mapping from `docker-compose.yml` on purpose ("a global name for a per-checkout thing") and states the intended path is `scripts/test.sh`, which execs pytest inside the stack where Postgres is `postgres:5432`. Host access is now opt-in via `COMPOSE_FILE=docker-compose.yml:docker-compose.dev.yml`, and only ONE checkout can hold 5432. So an agent following CLAUDE.md literally in a worktree hits `connection refused` and has no way to tell a deliberate change from a broken stack -- which is exactly what happened on 2026-08-27, costing a stretch of the session to diagnose. Three workable commands now exist (`scripts/test.sh`; the host command with `DENTALIA_TEST_ADMIN_URL`/`TEST_URL`/`TEST_API_URL` pointed at the container IP; or the dev overlay) and CLAUDE.md names none of them. Decide which is canonical and say so there, including what a second concurrent worktree should do.

- [x] 2026-08-27 [migration-041-collision] **MERGED 2026-09-02** — superseded by `[migration-041-collides]`, which describes the same two files AFTER the merge this one was written to gate. Its premise ("blocks the EUDAMED merge, decide before either branch lands") is spent: the merge landed and both files are on master (`041_eudamed_job_types.sql`, `041_vendor_import.sql`, verified 2026-09-02). Nothing broke, because `_migration_files` sorts on the full filename and both are additive. Original report below.
  `S · blocks the EUDAMED merge, decide before either branch lands` **Two migrations claim 041, on two different branches, and neither one errors.** Verified 2026-08-27: master holds `migrations/041_vendor_import.sql`; `worktree-eudamed-enhancement` holds `migrations/041_eudamed_job_types.sql` (branched off a master whose tip was 040) and 042-045 behind it. `app/db.py:44` is `sorted(glob("*.sql"))` and `run_migrations` tracks by `path.name`, so after a merge BOTH files apply and nothing complains -- but `041_eudamed_job_types` sorts alphabetically BEFORE `041_vendor_import`, so a fresh database applies them in the opposite order to the dev database, where `041_vendor_import` already ran and is already in `schema_migrations`. That is a silent divergence between "the schema a new environment gets" and "the schema we have been testing against", which is the one thing a numbered-migration convention exists to prevent. **It does not stop there:** renumbering EUDAMED to 042-046 then collides with `playbooks-into-db`, which already took **046** (`046_manufacturer_entity.sql`) and 047 -- that branch correctly reserved 042-045 for EUDAMED and assumed EUDAMED would keep 041. So the renumber has to be decided across all three branches at once, not on whichever merges first. Precedent exists and is cheap: `worktree-agent-aa85e6f3850460794` did exactly this ("migrations: renumber the lineage migration to 037"). **Also worth deciding while here:** whether `_migration_files` should refuse a duplicate numeric prefix outright rather than sorting through it -- a four-line guard that turns this class of collision from silent into loud, and there is no test for it today. Found by the 2026-08-27 drift check. **Cost so far, from `playbooks-into-db`:** that branch renumbered its own migration FOUR times (040 -> 041 -> 042 -> 043 -> 046) as other worktrees took each number in turn, and 043 ended up claimed twice anyway -- the renumber is not a one-off tax, it is paid again every time a neighbouring branch lands first.

The live question — whether the numbering is load-bearing enough to rewrite six files for — is carried by `[migration-041-collides]`. Kept as the record of the ruling, closed as a task. Original report below.
  `XS · fix it at merge time, not before — Denis, 2026-08-27` **RULED: a problem, but not a breaking one.** Denis: "we fix it when merging because this usually happens when two separate sessions are both creating migrations." So this is the expected cost of parallel sessions, not a defect to chase: whoever merges renumbers, and the renumber has to look at all three branches at once because `playbooks-into-db` already holds 046/047. Do NOT renumber pre-emptively on a branch that has not been asked to merge — that just moves the collision. Kept open as the note the merger reads, not as work. Original finding: **Two migrations claim 041, on two different branches, and neither one errors.** Verified 2026-08-27: master holds `migrations/041_vendor_import.sql`; `worktree-eudamed-enhancement` holds `migrations/041_eudamed_job_types.sql` (branched off a master whose tip was 040) and 042-045 behind it. `app/db.py:44` is `sorted(glob("*.sql"))` and `run_migrations` tracks by `path.name`, so after a merge BOTH files apply and nothing complains -- but `041_eudamed_job_types` sorts alphabetically BEFORE `041_vendor_import`, so a fresh database applies them in the opposite order to the dev database, where `041_vendor_import` already ran and is already in `schema_migrations`. That is a silent divergence between "the schema a new environment gets" and "the schema we have been testing against", which is the one thing a numbered-migration convention exists to prevent. **It does not stop there:** renumbering EUDAMED to 042-046 then collides with `playbooks-into-db`, which already took **046** (`046_manufacturer_entity.sql`) and 047 -- that branch correctly reserved 042-045 for EUDAMED and assumed EUDAMED would keep 041. So the renumber has to be decided across all three branches at once, not on whichever merges first. Precedent exists and is cheap: `worktree-agent-aa85e6f3850460794` did exactly this ("migrations: renumber the lineage migration to 037"). **Also worth deciding while here:** whether `_migration_files` should refuse a duplicate numeric prefix outright rather than sorting through it -- a four-line guard that turns this class of collision from silent into loud, and there is no test for it today. Found by the 2026-08-27 drift check.

The REF branch, `disposition = "production" if score >= cfg.gate.high and ref_gate and not _gates_disposition(flags)` (`app/handlers/gate.py:814`), never consults it, and no VALIDATE flag covers regime: the whole vocabulary (`no-item-identifier`, `multi-group-match`, `manufacturer-unresolved`, `no-ref-overlap`, `ref-catalogue`, `multi-manufacturer-ref`, `ref-unscoped`, `date-insane`, `downgrade-uncomparable`, `older-than-current`, `auto-superseded`, `expiry-on-certless-doc`, `cert-unresolved`) is about REF matching, dates, supersession and certificates, never about whether the document is evidence of a device at all. The gap in this entry is still REAL and still worth closing -- GATE's REF branch has no device check and the docstring admits it -- but the urgency was mine, not the evidence's. What makes it worth doing anyway: `coverage_scope` IS escalated and `needs_escalation` is recomputed after T1 (`tiers.py:304`), so a T1 answer of `group` (the natural reading of a document that names one article) reopens `ref_list` at T2 and the REF branch with it. The premise the flag was built on ("MDR says a DoC carries no expiry, so one is suspicious") is true of the regulation and false of this corpus: IVOCLAR prints real expiry dates on its declarations, GC does not. Proposal: keep it CAPPING when the `validity_to` evidence carries **no** explicit expiry phrase (the genuine defect — the GC signing-date bug that started this), demote to INFORMATIONAL when it does. Do NOT ship on the 110: that is one brand. Re-measure across all 12 corpus brands after the sweep and check the phrase list against the five corpus languages before it gates anything. Original report below.
  `S ~30m · runbook + a dump list` **A registry wipe silently takes `fetch_log` with it, and `fetch_log` is the one table that makes a re-derivation free.** `TRUNCATE document, item_document, evidence, manual_task, audit_log RESTART IDENTITY CASCADE` also empties `fetch_log`, because `fetch_log_doc_id_fk` references `document` — measured 2026-08-17 during the option-B rebuild: 330 rows gone, having been explicitly listed as KEEP moments earlier. Recovered at no cost only because `imports/` was still on disk and the ledger is derivable from the files (`url_normalized` = `str(path.resolve())`, `content_hash` = `sha256(bytes)`, `source='backfill'`); a re-hash of 330 PDFs reproduced 330 rows / 328 distinct hashes exactly, covering all 328 stored extractions. Two things to do: state in the runbook that any registry reset must `pg_dump` `fetch_log` alongside the five registry tables (or drop the CASCADE and clear `fetch_log.doc_id` first), and note that CASCADE's reach is `document`'s inbound FKs, which is not the list a person writing the TRUNCATE is thinking about. Related: `[backfill-not-regenerable]`, which is the same ledger seen from the other side — there, keeping `fetch_log` while deleting jobs is what strands a document; here, losing it is what would have cost a re-extraction.

The bytes are then used as parametrize arguments with no `ids=`, so the implicit test IDs differ between workers that import the module at slightly different instants. Hit twice in one session. Fix: pass explicit `ids=` to that parametrize, or build the PDFs in a fixture rather than at module scope.

This entry's value is its evidence and is folded there: three reproductions on 2026-08-31, a different subset of the three tests each time, plus the observation that the fixture does NOT take the cluster-wide advisory lock `tests/conftest.py:308-333` uses for its own create-and-migrate. Original report below.
  `S ~30m · reproduced 3x, cause still not established` **`tests/test_db.py`'s three `scratch_db` tests failed twice, then passed, and I could not find out why.** The fixture creates a database with the FIXED name `dentalia_test_migration_order` (`tests/test_db.py:143`), doing `DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS ... WITH (FORCE)` then `CREATE DATABASE` -- two statements, not atomic. Two runs in the scheduler-onto-queue worktree hit `UniqueViolation: Key (datname)=(dentalia_test_migration_order) already exists` at fixture setup, plus one `AdminShutdown: terminating connection due to administrator command`, which is what `WITH (FORCE)` does to another session's connection. That pair means two users of the same fixed-name database at once. **Checked afterwards and found nothing:** no pytest process in the container, no `dentalia_test%` database left on the cluster, no connections to it; the next full run was clean at 2682 passed. The worktree has its own compose project and its own Postgres, so a cross-worktree collision should be impossible, and `--dist loadfile` keeps the whole file on one xdist worker, so an intra-file race should be too. Both of those "should"s are now suspect. **Seen three times now (2026-08-31), with a different subset of the three tests each time**, so it is a live flake rather than a one-off -- and `test_db.py` is the only file that touches this name, so `--dist loadfile` should already be serialising them. Note the fixture does NOT take the cluster-shared advisory lock conftest uses for its own create-and-migrate (`tests/conftest.py:308-333`), which is the difference worth looking at first. Worth either a per-worker suffix on the name (the shared test DB already uses one -- `dentalia_test_26_580ed717`) or an advisory lock around the drop/create pair, the way conftest already serialises its own create-and-migrate.

  `XS · a name rule, not a client question` **`Dentsply IH` is three legal entities with two different roles, and treating the string as "not a manufacturer" would discard real ones.** Measured 2026-08-17 over all 1.303 readable corpus PDFs (PyMuPDF, first 4 pages): `Dentsply IH Limited` (Stonehouse, UK) appears in **41** files, in all 41 preceded by "Authorized UK-representative" — the post-Brexit UKRP, irrelevant to EU/MDR attribution, and every one of those declarations names its actual manufacturer on the same page (`Maillefer Instruments Holding Sàrl`, `Dentsply DeTrey GmbH`, with SRN). But `DENTSPLY IH AB` (Mölndal, SE) and `DENTSPLY IH Inc.` (Waltham, US) appear in **4 files each as certificate holders / production sites** on EC and QMS certificates. So any exclusion must match the full entity name, never the `Dentsply IH` prefix. This was question 4 of `docs/2026-08-13-vprasanja-za-dentalio.md`, **deleted 2026-08-17**: it claimed 66 files and claimed they would be left without a manufacturer, both false, and the roles are printed on the documents, so it was never Dentalia's to answer.
- [x] 2026-08-19 [t0-type-markers-confuse-subject-with-class] **VERIFIED DONE 2026-09-02** — all four proposed fixes shipped. `"quality management system"` is gone from `_TYPE_MARKERS` (`app/extract/t0_templates.py:61` carries the comment naming doc 420), `"eu certificate"` sits beside `"ec certificate"` (:72-73), `iso 13485` is the sole ISO signal, and confidence is position-scaled — `conf = 1.0 if i < _TYPE_TITLE_CHARS else _TYPE_MENTION_CONF` (:525). The item's "Not built" framing was the stale part. Original report below.
  `M ~3h` **Seven documents across five manufacturers are typed `ISO` from the phrase `quality management system`, and every one is wrong. Doc 420 is at `production`.** 248/260 (3SHAPE `MDR CE cert ...`), 420 (KOMET `BRASS_HZ ..._CVE_...`, production), 439 (KOMET `533213_Certificate-CE_MDR...`), 518 (NEODENT `neodent certifikat.pdf`), 316 (STRAUMANN `Certifikat Straumann (vsadki) MDR`) are all EC/EU certificates; 657 (VOCO `Declaration Art 22_VisCalor Dispenser`) is a declaration. Root cause: `_TYPE_MARKERS` conflates a document's SUBJECT with its CLASS. Doc 420's title is literally "EU Certificate Quality Management System" -- the phrase is real, at position 15, and the document is genuinely about a QMS; it is still a certificate. Two aggravating mechanics, both measured: the scan returns the first marker in LIST order found anywhere (`t0_templates.py:198`), so `quality management system` (index 10) beat `ec certificate` (index 11) at position 0 on doc 518; and a MENTION is read as an identity, so an IFU citing a DoC at character 33633 was typed DoC at conf 1.0 (NEODENT doc 520). `conf=1.0` means "an exact phrase matched", never "the type is certain" -- and the filename tier, which only runs when the text tier finds nothing, typed all four NEODENT documents CORRECTLY at 0.6. **Three fixes tested against the real files, 2026-08-19:** removing the marker fixes 6 of 7 and breaks 420 into `IFU@1.0` off boilerplate; position-wins alone fixes only 518; **drop `quality management system`, add `eu certificate` beside `ec certificate`, keep `iso 13485` as the only ISO signal, earliest-in-document wins, confidence scaled by position -> 7 of 7 correct**, with the four spot-checked ISO 13485 certificates staying ISO. Not built: it changes shared extraction logic and re-types a production document. Interacts with `_TYPE_SETTLED_CONF` (`t0_templates.py:348`), which gates the `group` deduction on type confidence, and with invariant 5, which supersedes on `(coverage subject, type, regulation)` -- a mistyped document can only ever chain against the wrong things.

- [x] 2026-09-03 [kpi-coverage-denominator] **CLOSED 2026-09-03.** K1 gained a fourth line — an unexpired Declaration of Conformity, same denominator as strict and device, a strict subset of both — on the status board and in `GET /api/kpi`, with the reasoning in `docs/specs/kpi.md` §3 note 5. Measured over the 4.265 confirmed MD items: any paper 4.254 (99,7%), device-regulation paper 1.684 (39,5%), a declaration of any age 1.461 (34,3%), an UNEXPIRED declaration **489 (11,5%)**. 11,5% is the honest figure, since lapsed evidence does not count as coverage (Denis, 2026-08-24); the gap to 34,3% is the 972-device expired-declaration pile `[completeness-card]` tracks, and PHASES.md quotes 34,3% only because it ignores expiry. Was: `M ~4h · defect inside DELIVERED scope, not new work` **The coverage KPI is reported on two delivered surfaces and the headline number is not defensible in front of the client.** `_kpi_board` (`web/app.py:1644`) feeds BOTH the status page and the read API `GET /api/kpi` (`web/app.py:3886-3889`) -- so it sits on both delivered surfaces, the web UI and the read API. Filed 2026-09-03 after a review briefly mis-shelved it as separate work; it is a **defect fix** and belongs in the build as new scope. The substance is PHASES.md `What is next` item 1, re-stated here because the defect register is where a defect belongs: 99,7% of devices are `covered`, **36,4% carry an article-level match**, **34,3% hold an actual Declaration of Conformity**, and **62,3% hold nothing but an ISO/QMS certificate** -- a claim about the manufacturer's quality system, not conformity evidence for the device. That is C16 working as ruled (one certificate, 2.567 Carl Martin items), which is exactly why a surface reporting 99,7% overstates. `_kpi_coverage` (`web/app.py:1476-1499`) already varies BOTH axes deliberately -- strict vs processed-scope denominator, any-paper vs device numerator -- so the machinery is there and the missing cut is **by document type**: a DoC denominator alongside the any-paper one, on the page and in the JSON, with the ISO/QMS-only population named rather than folded in. `docs/specs/kpi.md` needs the same row, and `docs/specs/read-api.md` is additive-only so the API gains a field and loses none. Do NOT ship a number to a client-facing report before this lands -- the 2026-08-31 delivery report already had to quote article-level coverage and explain why, which is the workaround, not the fix. **Amended 2026-09-03, after `/coverage` shipped and `[w11-no-doc-list-needs-one-definition]` was superseded into this entry.** Two things this entry did not say and must. (a) **It now has a second surface to reconcile with**: `GET /coverage` publishes three gap counts over the same `md_flag` population, and whoever builds the DoC cut here has to land on numbers that can be explained next to that page. (b) **The disagreement is on TWO axes, not one.** The doc-type axis is the one described above. The second is expiry: `_COVERAGE_HELD` does not filter `p.expires` at all, while `_kpi_coverage`'s ANY_PAPER/DEVICE_PAPER require `p.expires IS NULL OR p.expires >= CURRENT_DATE`. So `/coverage` counts an expired Declaration of Conformity as held and the KPI board does not. Measured 2026-09-03 over 4.265 `md_flag` items: **2.804 hold no DoC at all, 3.776 hold no UNEXPIRED DoC -- a 972-item gap**, driven by 20+ REF-list DoCs sharing `expires = 2026-05-04`. Decide the expiry rule explicitly, do not let it fall out of whichever query is copied.

Was: `XS ~15m` The FETCH browser-fallback tier cannot launch a browser in the current `dentalia-app` image. `pip install ".[fetch]"` resolves playwright unpinned (installed: **1.62.0**) while the base image is `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright/python:v1.61.0-noble`, which ships `/ms-playwright/chromium-1228` + `chromium_headless_shell-1228`. 1.62.0 looks for `chromium_headless_shell-1234` and raises `BrowserType.launch: Executable doesn't exist at /ms-playwright/chromium_headless_shell-1234/chrome-headless-shell-linux64/chrome-headless-shell`, with Playwright's own "please update docker image" banner. Reproduce: `docker compose run --rm --no-deps worker python -c "from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright; p=sync_playwright().start(); p.chromium.launch()"`. Launching the bundled 1228 build by explicit `executable_path` works, so only the version pin is wrong. Fix either side: bump the base to `v1.62.0-noble`, or pin `playwright==1.61.*` in pyproject. Found while sizing worker memory (a Chromium page costs ~250MB cgroup); no live impact yet because no domain is flagged `needs_playwright`, but the tier is dead until this is pinned.
- [x] 2026-09-02 [discover-query-exact-matches-slovene-label] **DONE 2026-09-02 — Denis ruled option (c): use the label only when it carries a real product name, unquoted; drop it when it is Slovene category prose. Never quote it.** `_query_for` (`app/handlers/discover.py`) no longer wraps `facts.label` in quotes at all, and a new `_carries_product_name` helper decides whether the label is included: it is dropped only when its leading token matches a measured stoplist of ~48 Slovene device-category nouns (`SVEDER`, `KLEŠČE`, `ROKAVICE`, ...) AND every remaining token is a recognised unit/quantity or a curated filler word (colour, size, sterility/material, marketing adjective, shape/direction, `HS` as Henry Schein's own redundant supplier-code suffix); anything else surviving that filter — a brand family name (`VARIOS`) or a bare catalogue/model number (`T63`, `558/6`) — keeps the whole label, unquoted. **Measured against the live `item_group.label` distribution (8208 labels, 2026-09-02):** 2260 labels (27.5%) lead with a category noun; of those, 2229 (98.6%) still carry a real product token and are kept, and only 31 (0.4% of all labels) are pure category prose and get dropped — e.g. `ROKAVICE L NEPUD. PREMIUM 100KOS HS`, `ZAGOZDE LESENE ORANŽNA 100KOS`, `KOFFERDAM SET`. The rule matches all four labels the diagnosis called out: `NASTAVEK VARIOS E15D` and `KOLENČNIK RDEČ TI-MAX X95L` keep (brand token), `SVEDER T63 - 1,5 / 6,0 MM` keeps (bare catalogue number, not filtered as a unit since it carries no unit suffix), `ROKAVICE L NEPUD. PREMIUM 100KOS HS` drops. TDD: 6 new tests in `tests/test_discover_handler.py` (never-quotes, drops-pure-prose, keeps-a-brand-token, keeps-a-clean-name, no-label, plus the site-restriction test) all failed for the right reason (quoted label) before the fix and pass after; the 2 pre-existing exact-string assertions at the old lines 643/652 were updated from the quoted form to the unquoted form, and the third referenced test (line 891, `test_a_path_scoped_domain_reaches_the_query_intact`) needed no change — it only asserts `site:` tokens, not quoting. Full suite green: 2746 passed, 5 skipped. **Live Brave query, one call, real production data** (`INSTITUT STRAUMANN AG`, group 80, label `STRAUMANN VELIKA KASETA ZA BL IN TL`): new query `INSTITUT STRAUMANN AG STRAUMANN VELIKA KASETA ZA BL IN TL declaration of conformity pdf` (no quotes) returned **10 candidates**, several genuine straumann.com PDFs. **Out of scope, unchanged:** the T1 ranker (`_default_rank`) is still unwired (`[discover-t1-ranking]`) — the search rung now produces candidates instead of zero, but nothing downstream of the query fetches or ranks them yet; `DISCOVER_HOLD` and job enqueueing were not touched. Was: `S ~1h · needs one ruling · found by running it` **DISCOVER's search query exact-matches Dentalia's own Slovene BC description, so it returns zero for every manufacturer, always.** `_query_for` (`app/handlers/discover.py:219`) builds `{manufacturer} "{label}" declaration of conformity pdf` and `facts.label` is the internal catalogue text (`ROKAVICE L NEPUD. PREMIUM 100KOS HS`, `SVEDER T63 - 1,5 / 6,0 MM`). The quotes make it an exact-phrase requirement against text that exists nowhere on the open web. **Measured live against Brave 2026-09-02** over six manufacturers holding no documents (HENRY SCHEIN, INSTITUT STRAUMANN AG, NEODENT, IMES-ICORE, INTERDENT, CEFLA): **0 results each**. The identical query with the quoted label removed returns 10 results with 5-7 direct PDFs, including a genuine `EU_Medical_Device` certificate on `straumann.com`. **This stacks in front of `[discover-t1-ranking]` and changes its priority:** the query returns an empty candidate list before the unwired ranker is ever reached, so wiring the ranker alone would have achieved nothing, which was the plan of record (S1.7 P3). It also explains why `discovery_log` has never held a single `search` row. Fix is small but needs a ruling on which: drop the quotes, drop the label for Slovene-labelled groups, or normalise the label to a manufacturer product name. Worth noting the label-free query is manufacturer-wide, which is exactly the granularity `mfr-scope` evidence wants.

Nothing to build.** Marked IMPORTANT on 2026-08-17 and it was, on the evidence available that day; the corpus has since been measured and the evidence does not support it. **Read off `document_text` on 2026-08-27: 809 stored texts, and exactly TWO exceed the 360.000-char budget** — the two Straumann IFUs that caused this entry (143pp/818.240 and 109pp/558.164). **Non-IFU documents over budget: zero.** 777 of 809 are under 20.000 chars and the mean is **7.059**, which is 2% of the budget. The guardrail stays and is what will surface that — `bounded_text` returns the dropped-character count and `llm._text_content` logs it as `extract.text_truncated`, so truncation is counted, never silent. **Do not remove it to tidy up; it is the trigger.** One caveat on the figures: two of the four largest texts (558.164 and 155.288 chars) have no `document` row — never gated, so they cost nothing today but would on a re-extraction. **Also settled here: the IFU exemption Denis was asked to choose already existed** (2026-08-18, one day after this entry was written), so the escalation list quoted below has been stale since; a confident IFU escalates for `['manufacturer', 'ref_list', 'basic_udi_di']` only, and those an IFU genuinely answers — refusing them would lose coverage, not save waste. Was: **CORRECTION 2026-08-27, and it invalidates the half Denis was asked to choose.** He ruled "exempt IFUs first, then size the rest from data" — but **the IFU exemption shipped 2026-08-18, one day AFTER this entry was written**, so the escalation list quoted below (`['validity_from', 'validity_to', 'cert_number', 'manufacturer', 'ref_list', 'basic_udi_di']`) has been stale for nine days and the ruling described work already done. `tiers.py:127-129` drops `_ESCALATE_CERTIFICATE` for a confidently-typed IFU via `_NO_CERTIFICATE_TYPES`. Measured 2026-08-27 rather than read: a confident IFU now escalates for **`['manufacturer', 'ref_list', 'basic_udi_di']`** and nothing else; the same blanks on a DoC still escalate for all six. **And the remainder is not exemptible on the same argument.** "An IFU carries no certificate and no expiry by nature" is true and is spent; what is left is item identity, and an IFU *does* name its product REFs — that is real linking evidence, so refusing to chase it would lose coverage rather than save waste. **So the whole of the remaining work is the second half**: the flat 360.000-char budget and the head-only window. Two distinct problems inside it, worth separating — cost (a per-type budget: a 3-page DoC needs no cap at all) and **correctness** (head-only means a `validity_to` on the last page of a certificate annex is unreachable, which is a wrong answer, not an expensive one, and it no longer has anything to do with IFUs since those stopped chasing dates). Measure what the remaining escalations actually cost, and size the per-type budget from that measurement rather than from a guess — which is the correction this entry already carries once, having predicted ~$0.25 for two IFUs that billed **$0.4155**, because 360.052 characters of Slovene tokenised to **147.289 tokens (2,44 chars/token)**, not the ~90k an English 4-chars/token assumption gives. Do NOT skip to lowering the flat number: 360k is sized so a genuine 40-page multi-product DoC still arrives whole, and cutting it to suit IFUs would silently truncate the documents the registry depends on. The head+tail window stays part of the second half, not the first — `validity_to` on the last page of a certificate annex is unreachable today. Guardrail already in place, do not remove: `bounded_text` returns the dropped-character count and `llm._text_content` logs it as `extract.text_truncated`, so truncation is counted rather than silent. Was: **IMPORTANT — Denis, 2026-08-17: "log it for now, but mark important".** `LLM_TEXT_BUDGET_CHARS` (`app/extract/pdf.py:81`) is one flat 360.000-character number for every document type, and the window it keeps is "the front of the file" for every one of them. That was the right shape for the 400 it was built to stop, and it is the wrong shape for what it costs. Measured the same day on the two STRAUMANN IFUs that forced it: 143 pages / 818.240 chars and 109 pages / 558.164 chars, both previously dead-lettered on `prompt is too long: 341460 tokens > 200000 maximum`, both now clipped to 360.052 chars ≈ 90k tokens. T1 is the only tier that sends text (`llm.py:196`; T2 is vision and already bounded by `MAX_VISION_PAGES`). **Then billed for real on 2026-08-17, which corrected the estimate upward by two thirds:** the two IFUs cost **$0.4155 together** — T1 $0.1428 and $0.1509, T2 $0.0619 and $0.0598 — against the ~$0.25 predicted. The error was the chars/token assumption: 360.052 characters of Slovene tokenized to **147.289 tokens (2,44 chars/token)**, not the ~90k an English ~4 chars/token estimate gives. So one capped IFU costs **~$0.15 on T1 alone, three times the ~$0.05 the whole rest of a document costs**, and the same measurement shows the budget sitting at 74% of the 200k ceiling rather than the 45% originally claimed. The cap made the IFUs safe, not cheap, and less safe than it looked.
      Two things make that spend avoidable rather than merely large. First, the fields the escalation is actually chasing are not spread through the file: after the IFU scope rule shipped, `needs_escalation` on both files returns `['validity_from', 'validity_to', 'cert_number', 'manufacturer', 'ref_list', 'basic_udi_di']`, and every one of those lives on the front matter or the back matter of an IFU, never in the 130 pages of clinical procedure between them. A 90k-token window over the front is paying to read the middle. Second, T0 typed both documents correctly from **1.290 characters of page 1** — the budget is a hundredfold what classification needed.
      The shape to build is a per-type budget plus a per-type window: a `type`-keyed budget map (an IFU is not a DoC and a 3-page DoC needs no cap at all), and head+tail selection rather than head-only, so `validity_to` on the last page of a certificate annex is reachable. Both need the type, which T0 has before any LLM call, so the plumbing exists. Do NOT do this by lowering the flat number — 360k is sized so a genuine 40-page multi-product DoC still arrives whole, and cutting it to suit IFUs would silently truncate the documents the registry actually depends on. Also worth settling in the same pass: whether an IFU should reach T1 at all, given it carries no certificate and no expiry by construction.
      Guardrail already in place, do not remove: `bounded_text` returns the dropped-character count and `llm._text_content` logs it as `extract.text_truncated`, so any truncation is counted rather than silent. Related: `[extract-economics]` (prices as constants) and `[gate-thresholds]`.

Blast radius measured before shipping: **14 manufacturer-scope filed documents** (11 ISO, 3 EC), not the 443 the filed count suggests — the other 429 are `group`-scope C15 filings on a different path, untouched. **One thing the ruling did not cover and the build had to:** both cases take the mfr-binding route, and the standing review sentence says approving "links it to every medical-device product from that manufacturer" — a lie when there are none, and a trap, because `gate.apply` bind-manufacturer refuses a zero-link bind ([gate-bind-zero-links]). So the task carries `reason: md-class-unknown` and `/staging` renders a different sentence for it. Measured the same day: **4.265 items carry `md_flag`, 11.693 do not**, so the blank case is the majority of the catalogue and filing on it would be filing on absence of data. What to build: in `_handle_mfr_binding`, `n_items = 0` must no longer imply `filed` when the manufacturer resolved confidently — it stages and raises the review task instead. Keep `filed` for the case it was built for (C15: we genuinely stock nothing from this maker and the class column is populated enough to say so), which means the two cases have to be told apart rather than collapsed — that is the whole of the work, and the discriminator is whether ANY item under those BC codes carries a non-null class, not whether any carries `md_flag IS TRUE`. Accepted cost, stated by Denis: a longer review queue now. Live blast radius today is 3SHAPE's 6 ISO/EC certificates against 197 items with zero MD flags; it grows with VOCO (292 PDFs), DENSTPLY (405) and STRAUMANN (13), all of which have 0 MD-flagged items for the same reason. Original entry: C16 auto-bind files a manufacturer-scope document as `filed` when its manufacturer resolves but the catalogue holds no MD-flagged item for it. That is right when we genuinely stock no devices from them, and wrong when the BC class column is merely unpopulated — Denis, 2026-08-17: "bc class is genuinely empty now", read here as a data gap rather than a statement that those items are not devices. Live blast radius today is small and known: 3SHAPE's 6 ISO/EC certificates, against 197 catalogue items with zero MD flags. It grows the moment VOCO (292 PDFs), DENSTPLY (405) or STRAUMANN (13) are imported, since all three have 0 MD-flagged items for the same reason. Two options once BC answers: treat an empty class column as unknown-not-absent and keep those documents `staged`, or file them and rely on `[filed-relink]` to bring them back when the column fills. The second is cheaper and already has a home; it just does not exist yet.

- [x] 2026-08-25 [openapi-reachable-with-any-api-key] `S ~1h · found while building the access policy, VERIFY the fix syntax` **`/docs` and `/openapi.json` answer 200 to a caller presenting ANY `X-API-Key` value, valid or not.** Two facts compose into it: (a) Caddy now skips Basic for any request carrying that header (spec §5, the price of "Basic OR a key"), and (b) FastAPI registers its docs/openapi routes as plain Starlette routes via `FastAPI.setup()`, which app-level `dependencies=[...]` do NOT reach — measured, not reasoned: a TestClient with `require_authenticated_user=True` returns `anon=200 service=200 bc=200` for `/openapi.json`, `/docs` and `/static/*`. So `web/access.py`'s default-deny table says staff-only for those paths and does not actually govern them. What leaks is the route/schema listing, not data and not credentials — but it contradicts the stated policy. Fix shapes: scope the Caddy header exclusion to the paths that need it (`not { path /api/* header X-API-Key * }` — group-negation syntax UNVERIFIED, check it compiles), or set `docs_url`/`openapi_url` to a staff-only path the app router actually owns. `/static/*` is the same mechanism and is deliberate — the BC item card needs its stylesheet. **CLOSED 2026-08-25.** `docs_url`/`openapi_url`/`redoc_url` are now None and both paths re-registered through the router, so the app-level guard reaches them; verified through a real Caddy on the dev stack: bogus `X-API-Key` -> 403, was 200. Fixing it surfaced a worse one in the same place -- Caddy's `header_up X-Forwarded-User {http.auth.user.id}` fired on requests that SKIPPED basic_auth and sent the literal unresolved placeholder, which is non-empty, so ANY `X-API-Key` value bought full staff access. Caddyfile now splits into two `handle` blocks (real username when authenticated, header stripped otherwise) and `access.trusted_user()` refuses brace-bearing values as a second layer.

The original question, kept for whoever reopens it: **Does packaging compliance belong in this registry at all?** PPWR is Regulation (EU) 2025/40, applying 12 Aug 2026, repealing packaging directive 94/62/EC. It regulates PACKAGING sustainability -- recyclability, recycled content, substances of concern, labelling, EPR -- and its declaration of conformity is evidence about the box, not the device inside. GC Europe told Dentalia on 2026-08-05 that they now publish one PPWR DoC per article on the same dealer portal as their MDR DoCs; 980 of them are archived at `<scratch>/gc-ppwr-2026-08-25/` (see `[gc-ppwr-archive]`). **The argument for NOT adopting:** three independent secondary sources agree that Art. 19 distributor duties are to verify producer registration (Art. 44), labelling (Art. 12) and manufacturer/importer identification (Arts. 15(5)-(6), 18(3)) -- holding or archiving the DoC is not among them, and the duty to draw it up and retain it (5 y single-use, 10 y reusable) sits with the packaging manufacturer. On that reading GC is being helpful, not discharging an obligation of Dentalia's, and ingesting 980 documents per manufacturer costs ~$41 each at the measured GC rate against a €10-40/month steady-state target. **The argument FOR:** Art. 19 also sets a "due care" standard and bars a distributor from making packaging available it believes non-conforming; keeping the supplier's declaration is defensible evidence of having exercised it. That is a legal call for Dentalia, not an engineering one. **Verification limit on the above:** the primary EUR-Lex text could not be retrieved (five attempts, WebFetch x3 and curl x2, all empty bodies) -- the Art. 19 content is from secondary sources that agree with each other, not from the regulation. Confirm against the actual text before acting on it commercially. **If the answer is yes**, it is a PRD change before any code: the `regulation` vocabulary, what `type=DoC` means when the subject is a package, and per-article coverage. **If no**, nothing to build -- but `[non-md-doc-reaches-production-via-ref]` still ships regardless, because these files reach the pipeline through the SFTP dump without anyone deciding anything.

- [x] 2026-08-21 [serving-mechanism-and-doc-names] **RULED AND BUILT 2026-08-25 (Denis's ruling, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-25-item-document-access-design.md`).** All three gaps closed. (1) Mechanism: **there is no public, unauthenticated tier.** The webshop backend fetches server-to-server and relays the bytes to its own logged-in clinics under its own origin; BC gets an item-card hyperlink carrying a shared static key. An HMAC per-item capability URL was designed and REJECTED -- a BC hyperlink is a computed field built by string concatenation, so BC cannot compute a signature, and with the shop relaying there is no public surface for a capability URL to protect (spec §0). (2) Display name: derived, not stored -- latest-revision `manufacturer` evidence + type + regulation + effective expiry, in `web/item_docs.py`, shared by both routes so they cannot drift. (3) Credentials: three caller classes in `web/access.py` (`staff` Basic auth, `service` `X-API-Key` from a rotatable `WEB_API_KEYS` list, `bc` `?k=WEB_BC_LINK_KEY`), default-deny route policy, `hmac.compare_digest` throughout, Caddy exclusions so the two non-browser classes reach the app that owns their credentials. Slice 2 (manufacturer catalogue + order batch) and rate limiting are logged separately below. Was: `M · needs Denis` The read API is deliberately minimal (S1.6; ruling "expose a read API from day one, wire nothing yet"): `GET /api/items/{item_ref}/documents` is `SELECT * FROM item_document_production` (`web/app.py:2910`) — type, regulation, dates, `archive_url`, nothing else. Three gaps stand between that and the stated end-use (webshop/BC/support asks for an item, gets JSON of links with names and types): (1) the consumer mechanism is undecided — direct exposure vs links relayed through the webshop or BC (Denis 2026-08-21: "not sure yet... this mechanism is not yet fixed"); (2) `document` has no display-name field, so the response cannot carry a human-readable name — derive one (manufacturer + type + validity year) or add a stored title; (3) auth is the Caddy basic-auth ingress only, no per-consumer credential story. A fourth defect is already tracked as [task4-cert-view] (the view serves raw `validity_to`, so the API returns a null expiry for a DoC whose date sits on its cited certificate) — not re-logged here. (1) blocks (2) and (3); nothing to build until the mechanism is ruled.

- [x] 2026-08-17 [latest-extract-rev-can-be-the-poorest] `— record only, nothing to build` **FALSE ALARM, resolved the same hour it was raised. Recorded so nobody spends an afternoon on it again.** The observation was real: exactly 2 of 314 content hashes have a latest `extract_rev` holding fewer fields than an earlier rev, both the Slovene GC safety data sheets (`gc-tissue-conditioner-powder-sds-slo.pdf`, `...-liquid-sds-slo.pdf`), whose rev 1 (`T0+T1+T2`, 08-12) and rev 2 (08-13) carry values while **rev 3 (`T0` only, 08-14 11:36) is `{}`** — and GATE reads `max(extract_rev)`, so `{}` is what it sees.
      The inference was wrong. `{}` is not a degraded replay shadowing good data; it is the 2026-08-13 change ("fold whitespace before matching a document-class marker") working as intended. pymupdf renders those pages double-spaced, so `Varnostni  list` never matched the `varnostni list` marker; once it did, both documents classified as `msds` and correctly short-circuited with no LLM call (`tiers.py:235`, behaviour dating to a 2026-07-06 commit). That commit measured the blast radius at the time: "exactly 2 documents change class, both the SDS this was reported for" — the same two.
      So the **later, emptier rev is the correct one**, and revs 1-2 were the error being corrected: full T1+T2 spend on a safety data sheet. Both documents are legitimately absent from the registry and should stay absent; do not re-enqueue them and do not "fix" the rev selection to prefer the deeper ladder, which would resurrect exactly the wrong extraction. The theoretical risk (a shallow replay shadowing a deeper one) has no instance in the corpus that is not this intended case.

An already-lapsed certificate is a different and worse problem than one lapsing next month, and the board should say so — a separate section or a status column, not a negative number in a field labelled "days left". Do not simply filter them out: 1.046 items sitting on a certificate that expired in May is the single most urgent thing the registry currently knows.

Reachability is the normal path, not a corner case: an EC cert with no REF list gets `no-item-identifier` and lands `staged` by default. Fix is read-side in all four places, `AND c.status IN ('production','superseded')`, matching the forward path. Opus verification rejected the handler-side alternative: gating back-resolution on status destroys fetch-order independence, because the certificate shape that most needs back-resolution is exactly the one that lands staged.
      **PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-08-14.** `report.py:36` is fixed -- the weekly report and the scheduler's expiry scan no longer inherit a rejected certificate's date. Mutation-checked both ways, since tightening to production-only is its own bug -- a superseded certificate was real and its dates still describe the period it covered. **STILL OPEN: the three `web/app.py` reads** (`:501` `/items` next_expiry, `:533` `/items/{ref}`, `:638` `/expiry`), left because a parallel session held the file uncommitted.
